## Claude

Both problems are alignment problems, not art-quality problems. I haven't changed anything yet.

**Ortolan ears.** Your read matches what the screenshot shows.
- The painted ears are already in the head art.
- The `Ears_Floppy` gene then adds a second pair as separate parts. They draw in a lighter, skin-tinted blue over the body, so the head looks like it has four ears.
- The handoff item `XENO_HEADS_VERIFY_BALD_AND_BLUSHING` flagged this as cosmetic and left it for you to decide.
- The same doubling affects the Nelvaanian grey side points (`Outland_EarsFlatPointed`), the Lasat ears (`Ears_Pointed`) and the extra big eyes.
- Wherever the head art already has the feature, the likely fix is to remove the gene's attachment from that species. That is a small XML change. Painted-in is the only way to keep your art's alignment, because the game's ear parts anchor to the vanilla head shape.
- Last night I fixed only the Ortolan east-facing head height, by shifting it 31 px down.

**Mimbanese width.** I measured the drawn width and height of each head PNG, and the profile is wider than the front:

| Facing | Width × height (px) |
|---|---|
| south (front) | 175 × 240 |
| east (side) | 238 × 237 |
| north (back) | 135 × 172 |

- A side view as wide as it is tall, and 36% wider than the front, is what you're seeing.
- I can't say what the game expects. I haven't measured the vanilla head textures, so that number is UNMEASURED.
- The fix would be to repaint or rescale the side view to the vanilla footprint. The head canvas tool `fit_head_canvas.py` currently only aligns each facing to a common bottom edge. That is also the cause of the Ortolan east shift. It has no width or ear-tip logic.

**Skin colour.** The game tints the painted head by the pawn's skin colour. It multiplies the two together, so a pre-coloured head gets shifted by the skin.
- Mimbanese: the first shot measured the red head at about 0.65 grey on a grey body. I fixed that by drawing the head with a black mask so the paint shows as painted. I also set the body skin to the head's average red.
- Ortolan: the head is still tinted. Blue paint multiplied by blue skin (`Skin_Blue` and `PaleAzure`) comes out darker than the body, which fits the deeper blue heads in your screenshot.
- Painted as-is means you get exactly your art but skin genes can't vary the head. That is fine for a single-colour species like these.
- Tinted means skin variety works, but the paint has to be near-neutral so the tint produces the right colour.

**What I'd do next:**
1. Strip the duplicate ear and eye gene parts from the four species.
2. Make the Ortolan head painted-as-is, the same as Mimbanese.
3. Measure the vanilla head extents, then file a head-fitting item that fits each facing to them. That item would add ear-tip handling and width limits, and repaint the Mimbanese side view.

I haven't filed or changed anything yet. Say go and I'll do steps 1 and 2 offline, then file step 3.

## Claude

You're right: painting the parts into the head is better than the add-on ears. The design pass is at `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry/design/RimStarWars/head_parts_painted_vs_attached_2026-10-20.md`, published as `757e8efd6`. Nothing is built or filed yet.

**Why painting wins**
- **Overlap:** painted ears can overlap the body too. The painted Ortolan ears already do in your screenshot, so overlap is not an advantage of add-ons.
- **Alignment:** the add-on ears are anchored to the vanilla head and ignore our head art. That is the mismatch you saw.
- **What add-ons still do better:** they can be toggled by gene, and the skin colour applies automatically. Neither matters for a fixed, hand-painted species.
- **Rule:** painted by default for ears, horns, crests, snouts and stalks. Add-on parts only for something separable that any pawn could wear. No species has one today.
- **Cleanup:** I'd strip the duplicate ear and eye genes from the four species. `Ears_Human` would sit in the ear slot so a random ear can't be rolled. I haven't verified that last point.

**Skin colour:** the game multiplies the painted head by the pawn's skin colour. For a single-colour species like Ortolan I'd draw the head as painted, the way Mimbanese now is. A mask that tints only the skin and ears is possible but untested. Neutral grey paint that the skin tints is the other option.

**Rakata eye-stalks:** paint them into the head. The constraint is the same as the Mimbanese side-view width: the head art needs a defined size envelope per facing. Eye add-ons couldn't follow the stalks or the facings. The Facial Animation mod is already excluded for these species, so painted eyes lose nothing.

**Helmets:** this is the open problem, and none of it is tested. Hats are drawn at the vanilla head size, so a wider head sticks out around them. The options, cheapest first:
1. Hide the headgear graphic on these species.
2. Make species-specific helmets.
3. Scale or offset the hat per head type, if a mod supports it. I haven't read Tabula Rasa for that.

**What this implies, in order**
1. Measure the vanilla head extents per facing, then define the envelope painted heads must fit. That one measurement fixes the Mimbanese width, the Ortolan height and the Rakata question.
2. Teach `fit_head_canvas.py` the envelope. It only aligns bottom edges now, which caused the Ortolan east shift.
3. Strip the duplicate genes and re-shoot the lineup.
4. Run one joint hat test, with a hat on a painted-ear pawn and on an add-on-ear pawn.

Say go and I'll file those four as ledger items and start on the first two. The Wave 4 agent is still running.

The top sits at 156–158, the chin at 356–358, and all three facings are centred at x 256. Narrow heads are 156 wide.
2. **The old tool's constants were wrong:** they came from donor heads, not vanilla. The real chin is 356–358 (not 365) and the east view is centred at 256 (not 270).
3. **How heads map to the screen:** every head is stretched over a 1.5×1.5-cell mesh, so drawn size is the bounding box as a share of the canvas, and nothing can draw past the canvas edge. Head position comes only from the body type; `HeadTypeDef` has no offset or scale per head type. Helmets use the same head mesh.
4. **Tabula Rasa:** `DefModExt_HeadTypeStuff` only swaps the shader; it has no offset or scale. Its per-head-type helmet textures (`DefModExt_ApparelAlts`) look switched off in 1.6, because the value they key on is never set in the DLL. That last point is untested live.
5. **All four species break the envelope** (ratios to vanilla):
   - **Lasat:** front width 1.40×, side height 1.34×.
   - **Mimbanese:** side view is 1.36× as wide as its front; back view is only 0.72× the front's height.
   - **Nelvaanian:** side and back heights 1.30–1.32×; side 1.28× its front.
   - **Ortolan:** side height 1.36×, and the trunk hangs 0.20 of a head-height below the chin.
6. **Envelope limits:** these are my proposed numbers (the vanilla base is measured), stored as data in `head_envelope.json` with a per-species override slot (Rakata's eye-stalks will need one). Width and height may be up to 1.25× vanilla, with at most 0.25 above the crown and 0.06 below the chin. The facings must also agree with the front (south) view: side and back within ±10% of its height, back within ±15% of its width, side at most 1.20× its width.
7. **What `fit_head_canvas.py` does now:** `measure`, `vanilla --write` and `check --report` (a blank or missing facing is reported as unmeasured, never passed). `fit` uses one scale for all facings, kept inside the envelope, with `--anchor crown` for trunk cases. The old command form still works. One scale can't fix facings that disagree (Lasat and Nelvaanian side views, the Mimbanese back), so those need a repaint or a ruling per species. New selftest: `selftest_fit_head_canvas.py`.
8. **Files:** the report (pinned) is `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\Transient\head_envelope_report_2026-10-20.md`. The durable doc is `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\design\RimStarWars\head_envelope_2026-10-20.md`, and I corrected two now-outdated "unmeasured" lines in the parent design doc. Both paths are in the read-only mirror, so they show up there after `./mirror sync`; until then they are under `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry`.</result>

## Claude

Both requests are under way, with two agents running. Nothing about the result is known yet.

**Textures by each biome's current intention**
- I told the world-texture agent to derive each biome's texture from its current design intent. That means the biome rulings and its specs, roster, light regime and terrain as they stand now, not just the old review-sheet palette.
- For biomes that have had no dev pass, it will say what it inferred and from what. Those textures will be provisional.

**Mountains that make sense**
- A new agent is on it in two phases.
- **Phase 1 (offline):**
  - It renders the hilliness and elevation layers and measures why the ranges read as arbitrary. The measures are whether mountains form connected chains, whether they follow elevation, and whether rivers start in highlands and run downhill.
  - It then designs a coherent mountain system for this planet and changes as few tiles as will make the ranges read right.
  - It publishes the design to `design/Jawa/worldbuilding/mountain_system_2026-10-20.md`, with before and after renders in `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\scratch\mountains\`.
- **Phase 2 (live):**
  - It applies the edits through the bridge only when the texture agent has finished with it.
  - It saves under a new name, `ASHKARR_DEV_START_2026-10-20_MOUNTAINS.rws`, and doesn't overwrite your dev start.
  - If the game is down or the bridge is busy, it files the plan as a ticket instead of waiting.

It follows your standing rulings. It edits the one map by hand-authored plan, with no generator, no seed sweeps and no alternative planets. It judges by looking at renders, and it doesn't write the world save file directly.

- **World textures:** each biome gets its own texture, from its current design intent. The agent also does the single batched restart, which now carries the Wave 4 list, the head-gene deploy and a live head re-shoot.
- **Mountains:** it designs and measures a coherent mountain system, and applies it live after the restart.
- **Factions:** Opus is thinking deeply about the factions. The agent will:
  - find the "faction that hangs around" problem you remember, citing what actually happened;
  - inventory every faction in the world and in the code;
  - recommend, for each of Tallmere Combs, Sacrilege Hunters and the Space Exchange, how to stop it raiding, trading and appearing in lists;

1. **The lingering problem:**
   - **Mechanism (1.6 source):** the game only removes temporary factions, and marks a faction defeated only when the player takes its last settlement. So a faction with no settlements can never be defeated. It still raids, trades and visits, because no vanilla arrival event needs a settlement. **Tallmere Combs is exactly that:** in all four dev saves with 0 settlements and no leader, which also fires a null-leader error every tick.

2. **Space Exchange:** it cannot be removed or zeroed. Odyssey re-creates it on every load when it is missing (and the Celestial Rogues with it), whatever the def says. The Wave 4 register blamed `requiredCountAtGameStart`; that was wrong and I corrected it. The three dev saves without it should gain it on their next load (from the source, not seen live). The fix is to hide it, forbid its raids, and strip it from Core's four orbital traders. Ships then arrive with no faction, so no trade or techprints are lost.
3. **Tallmere Combs:** our own def is now hidden, raids forbidden, off the Configure Factions page and never generated. It stays as a stub so the saves keep loading. Deleting it now would break all four saves. **Decision for you:** the Leaning Scrub farmer camps that used it now belong to the Homestead Defense League (vanilla `OutlanderCivil`). The other choice is no farmer camps at all.
4. **Sacrileg Hunters:** stays cut with Caravan Adventures (in no save, nothing orphaned). A guarded hide-and-forbid patch covers it if the mod ever returns.
5. **General guard:** a banned-faction list kept as data, a checker (`banned_factions.py`) that checks the repo, a save, or the game's def dump, and a selftest (17/17).
   - **Gaps it closes:** hidden alone does not stop raids, and distress-call quests have their own switch. The checker also fails if any of our files points at a banned faction, which is how the farmer-camp reference was found.
   - **Today's results:**
     - repo PASS;
     - the pre-deploy dump FAILs, as expected;
     - the saves show Combs active in all four and the Space Exchange in START.
6. **Filed:**
   - `UNWANTED_FACTIONS_SILENCE_NOT_DELETED` (needs bridge) replaces `COMB_EXCHANGE_FACTIONS_EXCISE_POLITELY`: deploy, check the new dump, then in each dev save mark both factions hidden and defeated and remove the Space Exchange's orbital station.
   - `COMB_HOLDS_DEF_RETIRE_AFTER_THE_REMAKE` (offline): delete the Combs stub once no kept save holds it.
   - The world re-make runbook now requires the save check to pass at roster audit.
7. **Not touched:** the bridge was held by the other FOUNDRY agent during my work, so I made no live edits. ## Claude

The planet was not tidally locked on the 324-mod list. I fixed it live and saved a locked copy, but the original dev save still loads unlocked.

**Tidal lock**
- **Evidence:** on load the planet type read `Default`, and a clock scrub on the home tile made the sun set (glow 0.68 → 1.00 → 0.68 → 0.00).
- **Cause:** with Worldbuilder loaded, which every full list does, Alien Worlds reads the planet type from the save's `worldPresetName`. `ASHKARR_DEV_START_2026-10-20.rws` was made on the PAINT list and doesn't have that field. The old canonical save does.
- **The ritual:** create the world from Worldbuilder's TidallyLocked preset, which writes `worldPresetName`.
- **Fix:** two bridge calls set the preset name and refresh the active planet type. Afterwards glow stayed at 0.7535 across +6h, +12h, +18h and +15 days, and Direct Sun stayed at 35.40° throughout.
- **Locked copy:** `C:\Users\Mandrake\AppData\LocalLow\Ludeon Studios\RimWorld by Ludeon Studios\Saves\ASHKARR_DEV_START_2026-10-20_LOCKED.rws`.
- **Still unlocked:** the autosaves and `XENO_HEADS_REVIEW_2026-10-20.rws` still carry `Default`. I told the mountains agent to build on the locked copy.

**Solar angle per tile**
- Direct Sun matches geometry on the tiles sampled (29.97° read against 30° expected on one).
- The mod's glow is fixed in time but doesn't follow geometry. Planet-wide, 448 night-side tiles read as day and 1,796 day-side tiles read as night. For example, the west terminator reads lit at 0.72.
- One home-tile glow of 0.7535 against 1.00 expected is unexplained.

**Temperatures:** stored values match the painted record on all 21,872 tiles and follow the ruled curve. There are two mismatches:
- **The Scald:** it is about 8 °C below the curve, at 36–53 °C against the sheet's 58–70 °C. It goes on the end-of-build paint list.
- **Seasons:** the tidal-lock def still swings ±15 °C by season on every tile. This is your call.

**Rings:** they are the mod RW - Planet Atmosphere, dropped in Wave 2 and absent from the 324, SHIP, DEV and PAINT lists. The ring config and texture are still intact, but the working copy sits in the workshop folder, where a Steam update could wipe it. The restore steps are in `PLANET_RINGS_RESTORE_DEBRIS_STILL_IN_ORBIT`. They go in with the next larger-list cold load, and no shipped list was changed.

**Owed**
- **`TIDAL_LOCK_FIX_PRESET_NAME_WAS_MISSING`:** decide which save is the dev start, and add a load-time guard so a full list can't load unlocked.
- **`SUN_GLOW_DECIDE_WEST_TERMINATOR_SUNBATHING`:** your card on whether light should follow geometry like Direct Sun does.

## Claude

The mountains are rebuilt live and saved as `ASHKARR_DEV_START_2026-10-20_MOUNTAINS.rws`, built on the locked save. All five agents have now finished. The agent has only looked at the globe near the colony, where the honeycomb is still heavy, so your look is the real test.

**What was wrong (measured)**
- The ranges were coherent bodies, not speckle: 86 mountain bodies against 1,774 for a random layout of the same tiles.
- The problem was where they sat. Every named range except Cratercrown was a ring around the substellar point, and the nightside belts varied only 3–5° across 30–80° of bearing. That is the bullseye pattern the world-building doctrine bans.
- Dew Horn doubled the Cratercrown ring.
- 149 Mountainous tiles sat directly against Flat land.
- There were 355 Impassable tiles where the ruling allows only the 63 on the Cratercrown crest.
- 30 of 106 river sources were on Flat land.
- The hill overlay covered 57% of the planet.

**What changed:** 3,699 hilliness edits and 34 elevation raises, at most +187 m.
- No settlement, river or biome tile was touched, and no river link changed its downhill direction.
- The nightside crag belts became 20 shorter ridges at varied angles, with foothills. The largest single mountain body there fell from 349 tiles to 28.
- Dew Horn became six spurs radiating from the Scald.
- Impassable is back on the Cratercrown crest only, at 62 tiles.
- No Mountainous tile now touches Flat land, and every river source is LargeHills or higher.
- 1,939 SmallHills tiles with no relief of their own became Flat. Overlay cover fell from 57% to 49% of tiles.
- Overall counts went from 7,912 / 6,035 / 3,698 / 2,424 / 355 to 9,721 / 5,236 / 3,785 / 1,620 / 62 (Flat / SmallHills / LargeHills / Mountainous / Impassable).

**Checks**

- The lint findings were 32 before and 32 after, identical per check.
- The planet type read TidallyLocked before and after.
- The new save matches the plan with 0 differences offline.
- `ASHKARR_DEV_START_2026-10-20` was not overwritten.

- **Overlay mods:** whether World Map Enhanced and GRimTerra Worldmap come back is cosmetic, so it needs your permission.
- **Rock landmarks:** 66 Caverns and 33 Valleys are no longer on Mountainous tiles. The world already had 125 Caverns and 101 Valleys on Flat, and lint did not change.

## 2. Key Technical Concepts

Skin shader multiplies painted PNG by skin colour (Mimbanese measured ≈0.65 grey); Tabula Rasa `DefModExt_HeadTypeStuff shaderType CutoutComplex` + all-black masks = draw as painted. Every head is stretched over a 1.5×1.5-cell mesh; `HeadTypeDef` has no per-head offset/scale; Tabula Rasa `DefModExt_ApparelAlts` looks disabled in 1.6 (untested live).
- Vanilla head extents (42 Core heads, 512 px canvas): south/north 184×200, east 200×200, top ~156–158, chin 356–358, centred x=256. Proposed envelope: ≤1.25× vanilla, ≤0.25 above crown, ≤0.06 below chin, facings agree with south (side/back height ±10%, back width ±15%, side ≤1.20× south width). All four species (Lasat, Mimbanese, Nelvaanian, Ortolan) violate it.
- Rimflow ledger (sharded: `infrastructure/state/ledger/events/FOUNDRY.jsonl`), item prose in `infrastructure/state/items/<ID>.md`; names `SUBJECT_INTENT_TWIST` (3–7 words, ≤44 chars, `rimflow namecheck`); provenance flags `--owner-said / --by-card / --per-ruling / --why`; commit explicit paths, `./publish` prints PUBLISHED sha; file writes and commits in SEPARATE Bash calls.

- Tidal lock mechanism: with Worldbuilder loaded (every full list), Alien Worlds reads planet type from the save's `<worldPresetName>`, NOT `<alienWorldsFrameworkPlanetType>`. Fix live: `jawa/static_call set_WorldPresetName TidallyLocked` then `UpdateActive`. Direct Sun is a pure function of tile geometry (35.40° at the home tile, unchanged by time).

Grey Sea, Twilight Sea and Scald point back at the vanilla ocean texture, and the Chill goes back to its own pre-10-18 sediment-water art. I copied no art from other mods. The navy you saw in September came from World Map Enhanced, which is no longer on the list.

## Claude

The live audit found what causes the honeycomb, the rings and the grey seas. I sent you two images. The fixes are committed (`5dac40dda`) but not deployed, so you won't see them in game until the next restart.

**Honeycomb and grey hexes.** Our sea-floor layer sits at exactly the same size and position as the surface layer. The game draws a layer's terrain and hill pictures even when you aren't viewing it, so the two compete on every tile and the floor often wins.
- **What you see:** the grey hexes with circles are the floor's bedrock texture. The olive striped fields are its placeholder floor texture. The floor also adds a second set of hill pictures.
- **Proof:** the agent repainted a surface tile and its neighbours bright red and nothing changed on screen. It reverted that.
- **Wrong earlier guesses:** the research file blamed the vanilla hill pictures, and yesterday's commit blamed the ice shader on the desert. Neither is the cause.
- **Fix:** a small patch in Diving Interaction hides whichever of the two layers you aren't viewing.

**Grey seas.** The game only gives the ocean look to vanilla Ocean and Lake, so all our seas got the land look, which also flattens their depth to 0 m.
- **Fix:** our water biomes now use the ocean shader. Grey Sea, Twilight Sea and Scald point back at the vanilla ocean texture. The Chill goes back to its own sediment-water art from before 10-18. No art was copied from other mods.
- **Navy seas:** the navy you remember from September came from World Map Enhanced, which is no longer on the list.

**Rivers and roads.** The live world held the hand-made record, plus 983 river links and 489 road links left over from vanilla world generation. I cleared all links live and re-imported the record with 0 refused. It is saved as `ASHKARR_DEV_START_2026-10-20_RECORDLINKS`.
- **Little visible change:** the look barely changed on screen. Lint findings fell from 32 to 21.
- **Side effect:** some hills the mountain pass raised for leftover river heads now have no stream.
- **Roads into thin air:** roads still stop dead at the substellar desert. 196 of the record's own road links cross the Stillsand, whose biome flag hides roads.

**Restart needed.** At shutdown, run `deploy_custom_mods.py --compose biomes --apply`, because the mod DLLs can't be replaced while the game runs. That deploy also carries the banned-factions files. Then load `RECORDLINKS` and check `Player.log` for the line `world ocean shader on N water biomes`. I filed the check as `SEABED_BLEED_VERIFY_SEAS_WET_AGAIN`.

**Not investigated:** oversized feature labels and icon clutter (about 140 world objects, plus a mutator icon on each of 14,397 tiles). They are noted in the audit.

<result>World Map Enhanced is textures only. Its Workshop folder holds just `About/` and 229 PNGs (31 MB), with no Defs, Patches, Assemblies, shaders or settings. It overrides vanilla and other mods' biome faces by file path (`World/Biomes/*`, 512 px, plus a 1024 `Ocean.png`) and ships Biomes Kit hill and forest decal sprites (173 files, 256 px). It does not touch rivers, roads, `World/Hills*`, mutators, feature labels or WorldLayers. Its ocean has no depth gradient: it is flat navy (52,63,85) with faint horizontal ripple streaks. The depth look came from vanilla's WorldOcean shader. Our seas were using WorldOcean's texture path but not its shader, which the 2026-10-20 audit fix gives them (built, deploy owed).

GRimTerra World Map (Workshop 3546956014) is textures plus two small patch files, again with no code. One patch repoints vanilla AridShrubland and Tundra textures unconditionally. The other adds Biomes Kit controls to nine GRimTerra biomes that we don't use. Its faces are 4 to 10 times higher contrast and read blotchy at planet scale. I did not inventory its two sibling mods (`GRimTerra.Biomesmod`, `GRimTerra.TerrainRetexturemod`), since they aren't world-map art.

Why the old look worked: faces are near one flat colour (large-scale variation about 1 to 2 levels), detail sits at 4 to 30 px so it reads as grain, each biome is a single muted hue family, and every face tiles invisibly. Our current faces are 62 procedural placeholders with bricks, voronoi cells, rings and dots that repeat as visible stamps. I couldn't confirm vanilla's UV scale, mipmapping or border blending. No decompiled source is on disk, so those are marked UNMEASURED; RimSage or a cold-load look can settle them.

Spec for our faces:
- **Format:** 512x512 opaque 8-bit RGB (water may be 1024), seamless on both axes, one file per BiomeDef at `Textures/World/Biomes/&lt;defName&gt;.png`, twins sharing art. Hill and forest decal sets are optional (256 px RGBA).
- **Detail and contrast:** per-channel sd 3 to 9, large-scale blockiness 2.5 or less, features 4 to 30 px with soft edges. No isolated rings, dots, bricks or cells, because they repeat as stamps at planet scale.
- **Palette:** one hue family per biome taken from `design/Jawa/worldbuilding/biomes/the_*.md` and `Transient/biome_worldtex_2026-10-18/palette.json`, at 30 to 60% saturation and value 60 to 170 (ice and salt flats up to 200 to 230). Neighbouring biomes must differ in hue or value, and no saturated primaries.
- **Water biomes:** colour and ripple only, no depth baked in, since WorldOcean supplies depth. Use a calm cool dark field with sd 3 to 5 and one-direction streaks 15 to 40 px long, with no shore gradient or vignette. Means are roughly Grey Sea (75,95,110), Twilight Sea (40,70,105), Scald (55,120,130) and Chill (45,65,95), none brighter than about 140.
- **Checks:** run a seam and statistics test by script, tile 3x3, then look at it in game at three zooms including the borders between neighbouring biomes. Never copy WME or GRimTerra bytes; the owner must approve the change, since cosmetic changes need his card.

Report, pinned and published as `45048d4ea`: `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry/Transient/wme_analysis_2026-10-20.md`. Stats and contact sheets are in `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\scratch\wme\`.</result>

## Claude

World Map Enhanced is textures only. Its folder holds 229 images and nothing else: no defs, patches, code, shaders or settings. It doesn't touch rivers, roads, hills, mutators or labels.

- **Its ocean:** it is a flat navy field with faint ripple streaks. The depth gradient you remember came from vanilla's ocean shader. Our seas never got that shader, which is the fix now waiting on the restart.
- **GRimTerra Worldmap:** textures plus two small patch files, again no code. Its faces are much higher contrast and blotchy at planet scale.
- **Why the old look worked:** each biome was close to one muted colour. Detail was fine grain, and every face tiled invisibly.
- **Why ours looks bad:** the 62 placeholder faces I made have bricks, cells, rings and dots that repeat as visible stamps.

<result>I could not prove the source of the null textures offline, and I changed no game or mod files. The only commit is the ledger entry for the open question (`b3482a134`, published as `c6ed8ba76`).

**Null textures.** The log has no stack traces, so this is partly inference.
- The warnings are `GUI.DrawTexture` null-texture calls, which are UI draws, not world meshes.
- The current `Player.log` has 77: 29 at startup, 44 around the planet-shader load errors, and a few at world load.
- The 29 startup warnings are just after two "resource from a different thread" errors and just before the `RimThemes NX rev10` line. That equals the 29 loading-screen images in `Config\VanillaBackgroundsExpanded_LoadingSettings.txt`, which looks like Vanilla Backgrounds Expanded or RimThemes drawing a texture that came back null off the main thread. I did not confirm this.
- `Player-prev.log` shows the flood: 19,519 warnings, with two runs of about 10,000 starting right after the world-ocean "maskTex" lines, until the log cap hit. So it is per-frame drawing, and a longer stuck load means more lines.
- The two cross-thread errors name vanilla textures: `UI/Commands/TryReconnect` and `UI/Designators/Cancel`. I did not check whether other vanilla gizmo icons are also left null.
- I ruled out faction heads: all 12 settlement factions have an icon path, and the PNGs are in `UtinniPatches`. The infestation site icon `BI/HiveColony` also exists, and the asteroid defs use vanilla icons.

**Labels.**
- A label's size is its `maxDrawSizeInTiles` run through the vanilla curve (10→15, 25→40, 50→90, 100→150, 200→200) and multiplied by tile size. The curve clamps at 15, so no label can be smaller than 15 tiles wide.
- In `WorldFeatures.GoodCameraAltitudeFor`, labels sized above 1 stay visible at mid zoom (altitude percent of 0.35 or more). Big regions therefore stay large exactly when zoomed to mid range.
- The canonical save now holds the curve I found in `world_label_curve.py`: values run 10–61, median 18, with the Fall Line at 26. To shrink labels, lower `K` (1.35) or cap the maximum in that script and re-apply it to a new save slot. That is an owner decision made by looking at the globe.

**Clutter.**
- The save has 96 settlements (27 Homestead League), 20 infestation sites and about 83 asteroid parents: roughly the 140 world objects you counted.
- The icon on each mutator tile comes from Mutator World Icons (`Snobi.MutatorWorldIcons`). Its settings file `Mod_3551904193_MutatorWorldIconsMod.xml` does not exist, so it runs on defaults: icon size 1.15 and visible at "Close" zoom or nearer, so it should not show at mid zoom.
- The cleanest proposal is to untick "Toggle Animal and Plant Icons" in the world toolbar. Otherwise write that file with `isEnabled` false, or `iconSizeModifier` 0.5. The file name pattern is inferred from the one other mod file in `Config\`, and I did not write it.
- Landmark pictures (3,008) are tile decals with no toggle.

**Still owed.**
- The null-texture caller needs a game-up probe, filed as `WORLD_NULLTEX_FLOOD_HUNT_STACKLESS_SO_FAR`. The probe would hook `GUI.DrawTexture` and log one stack on the first null.
- The label-size lowering needs your decision.
- The Mutator World Icons setting needs whoever holds the game to apply it.
- The ledger edit to `OWNER.jsonl` that was already in the tree is not mine and I left it alone.

Scratch is in `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\scratch\worldlook5`.</result>

## Claude

The log-noise tracer finished, and I started a replacement so three agents are running again. It could not prove where the null-texture flood comes from, and it changed no game or mod files.

**Null textures**
- **What they are:** the log has no stack traces. The warnings look like UI draws, not world meshes.
- **Likely source:** 29 of the 77 appear at startup. That matches the 29 loading-screen images in the Vanilla Backgrounds Expanded config, so Vanilla Backgrounds Expanded or RimThemes is the likely culprit. It is unconfirmed.
- **The flood:** the previous log had 19,519 of them in two runs of about 10,000, until the log cap hit. It is per-frame drawing, so a stuck load makes more lines.
- **Ruled out:** faction icons. Every settlement faction has one.
- **To pin it down:** it needs a probe in the running game. That is filed as `WORLD_NULLTEX_FLOOD_HUNT_STACKLESS_SO_FAR`.

**Feature labels**
- **Mechanism:** each label's size comes from a vanilla curve that clamps at 15 tiles wide. Big regions stay large at mid zoom.
- **Fix:** lower the scale factor in `world_label_curve.py` (currently 1.35) and re-apply it to a new save slot.
- **Decision:** that is your call by looking at the globe, so I'm leaving it.

**Icon clutter**
- **Source:** the icon on each of the 14,397 mutator tiles comes from Mutator World Icons. It runs on defaults and shows only at close zoom.
- **Proposal:** switch it off in the world toolbar, or write its settings file with `isEnabled` false. The settings file name is inferred, and the live agent hasn't applied it.
- **Landmarks:** the 3,008 landmark pictures have no toggle.

The new agent is working out why the glow on the map doesn't follow the sun geometry. The three now running are the live border/sea/factions agent, the second mountain planner and this one.

<result>The tidal lock does work once it is switched on. The day/night mismatch comes from the borrowed tidal-lock mod's own glow formula, not from anything we ship. The odd 0.7535 on the home tile was a stale cached reading in our measurement, not a real deviation. Committed and published `50e39a3ef`. I wrote no gameplay fix, because both remaining fixes are the owner's decisions.

**1. What computes the glow.** When the lock is active, `7f.alienworlds.tidallylocked` (`C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\workshop\content\294100\3631364335\Source\PlanetTypeDef.cs`) replaces the game's per-tile glow calculation (`CelestialSunGlow(PlanetTile,int)`) with one that reads only the tile's position, never the clock. It also pins the sun's day-of-year and time-of-day, and turns off the daily temperature swing. So the sun cannot change with time once the patches are on. The light is computed from the planet's fixed sun position (latitude 0, longitude 0).

**2. Why the light doesn't follow geometry.** The mod passes the tile's longitude into two vanilla routines written for latitude:
- **Polar-winter tilt:** drops the sun 11.3°, and in the mod's frame that drop points west. The west rim stays lit past sunset and the east rim goes dark while the sun is still up.
- **"Sun peeks around":** bends the sun 19° toward the tile, plus up to 17° more inside 60° longitude.

The result is then capped with `clamp01(dot/0.7)`. The mod's own comment calls this "hacky". Our earlier offline copy of the formula matched the live game to 3 decimals on four freshly generated test maps. I checked our own glow multiplier: the home biome (`RUT_ExtremeDesert`) doesn't carry one.

**3. The home tile's 0.7535 was a stale cache.** That number is exactly vanilla's glow at the "+15 days" sample taken before the fix (I recomputed it offline to 4 digits). The Performance Optimizer mod caches the per-map glow reading, and after the clock was set back to tick 3, every read returned that old value. So the earlier "glow constant at five clock times" was not proof of the lock. The lock is proven by the mod's patches being present and by the fresh test maps. Under the lock the home tile reads 1.00.

**4. It matters for gameplay.** Direct Sun covers heat only. Glow decides whether it counts as daytime (sleep, animal schedules), plant light, solar power, darkness mood and accuracy, and shadows. On the two rims (about 1,796 day-side tiles reading night, 448 night-side tiles reading day), heat and light disagree.

**5. Seasons (±15 °C).** These come from vanilla's seasonal temperature code. Alien Worlds redirects it to the tidal-lock planet's `seasonalTempVariationCurve` (15, 15, 5), so every tile swings 15 °C, opposite on each side of the equator. A one-file XML patch on that curve, guarded so it only applies when the tidal-lock mod is loaded, would remove or shrink it. I described it in the item but did not write it, since that's the owner's call.

**6. The Scald.** It was not on the end-of-build paint list, so I added it to `BIOME_PAINT_ONCE_AT_THE_END_1`.

**Files changed:**
- The audit (`D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\design\RimMandrake\world_physics_audit_2026-10-20.md`): wrong §5 claim corrected, mechanism added.
- New entry for the cache trap in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\infrastructure\state\facts\instrument_traps.md`.
- The three items rewritten with state, evidence and `NEXT:`, plus a two-sentence card framing in each decision item. Rimflow notes filed on all three.

**Still needed:**
- **Bridge:** `TIDAL_LOCK_RESTORE_SUN_STILL_COMMUTING` needs one clock test that avoids the cache, run after the fix item (`TIDAL_LOCK_FIX_PRESET_NAME_WAS_MISSING`) lands. Call the glow function with explicit times, or only move the clock forward.
- **Owner cards:**
  - `SUN_GLOW_DECIDE_WEST_TERMINATOR_SUNBATHING` — keep the mod's lopsided light, or add our own switch so light follows the sun angle.
  - `LOCKED_SEASONS_DECIDE_FIFTEEN_DEGREE_WOBBLE` — keep, remove, or shrink the seasons.

## Claude

The sun-glow agent finished, and its answer mostly clears the lock. The tidal lock works once it is switched on. I started a world-parity audit as the replacement, so three agents are running again.

**What the agent found**
- **The lock works:** with the lock active, the borrowed tidal-lock mod replaces the game's per-tile light calculation with one that reads only the tile's position, never the clock.
- **Where the day/night mismatch comes from:** the mod's own light formula feeds the tile's longitude into two routines written for latitude. The west rim stays lit past sunset and the east rim goes dark while the sun is still up. About 1,796 day-side tiles read night and 448 night-side tiles read day. Our earlier offline copy of the formula matched the live game to three decimals on fresh test maps.
- **The odd 0.7535 reading was our measurement error, not a real deviation.** A performance mod caches the per-map light reading, and it kept returning an old value after the clock was reset. Under the lock the home tile reads 1.00.
- **It matters:** heat comes from Direct Sun, but light decides daytime, sleep, solar power and mood. On the two rims, heat and light disagree.
- **Seasons:** the ±15 °C swing comes from the tidal-lock planet's own seasonal curve. A one-file patch guarded on that mod could remove or shrink it. The agent didn't write it.
- **The Scald:** it was missing from the end-of-build paint list, and the agent added it.
- **Published:** `50e39a3ef`, with the corrected audit, a new entry in the instrument traps file, and the three items rewritten.

**Two decisions for you**
- **Light lopsidedness:** keep the mod's lopsided light, or add our own switch so light follows the sun angle.
- **Seasons:** keep, remove, or shrink the ±15 °C swing.

**One bridge check still owed:** a clock test that avoids the cache.

The parity agent is building a measured table of the canonical start against the dev world: tiles, rivers, roads, world objects, features and settings. Anything it can't explain gets ranked by how visible it is to a player. The three running are the live border/sea/factions agent, the second mountain planner and the parity audit.

<summary>Agent "Offline: why atmosphere shader fails" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>The "not supported on this GPU" errors are harmless, and the missing limb glow is a separate problem that I could only narrow down offline. I did not run it live. PUBLISHED `33cd4b1f965e337e6d566e4f07e3ddb193aac943`.

**Why the errors appear.** `Player-prev.log` (the run with Rings) has 13 pairs of `Desired shader compiler platform 4 is not available in shader blob` and `Shader Planet/&lt;name&gt; … not supported on this GPU`. The shaders named are SunFlare, Tonemaps, WriteDepth, RemoveAlpha, AddToTarget, AtmosphereLUT, TranslucentGenrater, OutSunLightLUTGenrater, InSunLightLUTGenrater, ScatterGenrater, CopyToDepth, and `Custom/BasicRing`.

- **Cause:** the GPU is fine (RTX 5080, D3D11 level 11.1, Unity 2022.3.35). RimWorld's bundle loader only recognises the suffixes `_win`, `_mac` and `_linux`. The mod's bundles are named `shader_atmosphere_windows`, `_macos` and `_linux`, so both the Windows and the MacOS bundle load.
- **Effect:** the MacOS bundle holds Metal shaders only (platform 14). Unity complains about those, and the mod's `GetShader` skips them and takes the D3D11 copy from the Windows bundle. There is no `Unable to load shader` line anywhere in the log, and the ring draws, which agrees with this.
- **What the log can't show:** I did not trace which bundle loads first, so I'm inferring this from the missing `Unable to load shader` line and the working ring.
- **Fix:** nothing needs fixing. The errors only go away if the MacOS bundle is removed from the workshop copy, which I won't touch.

**Why the limb glow may be missing (hypothesis).**
- **Likely cause:** the live config's atmosphere shell is `minh` 60.21 to `maxh` 68.71 in world units, but `planetRadius` is 100, so the shell sits inside the planet. The mod's default `defaultAtmosphere` is 100.0 to 100.8. My guess is that the config was hand-tuned for a 63.7 radius.
- **Not proven:** the September "thick blue scatter" snapshot suggests the glow did show then. In the one September limb screenshot I opened (`D_limb_drama_clean.png`) only a faint cool fringe on the left limb is visible.
- **Vanilla fallback:** the mod's patch already removes the vanilla `WorldDrawLayer_Glow`, so the mod's atmosphere is the only limb glow, and its settings file is where to tune it.

**Settings.**
- **File:** `Config\Mod_3272330410_AtmosphereMod.xml`. There are no on/off toggles.
- **Ring:** the `TransparentObject_Ring` entry controls it (`ringMapPath`, `ringFromTo`, `opacity`).
- **Atmosphere:** the `TransparentObject_Atmosphere` entry controls it (`minh`/`maxh`, scatter, exposure, scale heights).
- **Global:** `gamma`, `refraction`, `luminescen` and `sunColor` apply to both.
- **Format:** values are stored multiplied by 100000.

**Smallest fix, and a live check.** I added a test config at `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\deployed\config\Mod_3272330410_AtmosphereMod.LIMB-TEST-shell-above-surface.xml` (WSL `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry/deployed/config/`). It is the `TRUE-GLORIOUS-RING` config with only `minh` changed to 10000000 and `maxh` to 10800000, so the shell sits just above the surface. A live check needs the bridge and the larger list with Rings:
1. Take an orbit-zoom screenshot with the current config.
2. Swap in the test config and take the same screenshot.
3. Compare the limb in the two frames.

**Memory.** The with/without-Rings readings are still owed. They need two loads with the working set and private bytes recorded, one with `rwnodetree.rwplanetatmosphere` and one without.

**Ledger.** I filed `ATMOSPHERE_LIMB_SHELL_PROBE_UNDER_GLOBE` (needs bridge) with a `NEXT:` note covering both checks. The ledger event is committed and published with the config.</result>

**Region labels (U1).**

- **With the right formula:** the new world has 0 of 63 labels on the wrong ground, and the old canonical save had 0 before 2026-10-09. The 2026-10-09 "fix" moved 60 canonical labels off their regions, so 51 are now off. Don't run that tool's `--out` fix on the dev world, because it would move 52 correct labels onto the wrong ground. The tool is corrected.
- **Sizes did regress:** the stamp computes `sqrt(tiles)×1.35`. The plan restores canonical sizes for all 71 labels by name, 68 of them changing (Fall Line goes 16.8 to 26). It also moves each label to its region's middle. The label-scale question is untouched.

**Mutators (U2).**
- **Confirmed:** 28,616 dev entries were never written by the stamp. The engine rolled them at world generation, for example 2,064 Mountain entries, 1,124 of them on flat tiles.

Recorded as a general rule, not just a head rule. Alignment, crop, centre and scale steps must register on the rigid body (skull, torso), never on the image extremes. Otherwise the shape or motion of an appendage leaks into where the body sits.

It audits every tool that aligns, crops, centres or scales the flier flip-book frames for bounding-box registration. It measures how much the body shifts between frames for each shipped flier set and reports which ones are off. It makes no repaint and no live flight test, since the owner has to be present for those.
- **Head refit item:** `HEADS_REMEASURE_ON_SKULL_NOT_EAR_TIPS` stands as filed. I fixed a wrong item name in the memory note.

<result>I did all four jobs plus your two follow-ups, all on one world. Biome borders now fade instead of meeting as hard hex lines, and seas have a pale shore band and darker deep water. Everything is in one new save, `ASHKARR_DEV_START_2026-10-20_PARITY.rws`. Code is pushed in `4d3d87e64` and the audit doc and ledger in `79f95ccf1`.

**What changed**
- **Biome borders:** where two land biomes meet, the game now draws each tile again with its neighbour's texture, fading in toward the edge. The colour matches from both sides, so the hex line becomes a gradient about one tile wide. The code is `src/RimMandrake/BiomesShell/Source/RM_WorldLook.cs` in Baroque Biomes.
- **Seas:** vanilla's ocean shader turned out to ignore depth completely. I fed it depths from 0 down to -100,000 m and the pixels did not change, so depth can't come from the shader. Instead each water tile gets two thin coloured layers: shallow teal fading out over about one tile from the coast, and deep navy fading in over four. No tile elevations or textures were changed.
- **Settings:** both effects have an on/off switch in Mod Settings, on by default, taking effect at once.

So each water tile now gets two thin layers instead: shallow teal that fades out over about one tile from the coast, and deep navy that fades in over four. No elevations or textures were changed.

<result>I've rebuilt the head fitter so it sizes and seats every head on its skull, not the outer box, and refit all four species from the original art. The Ortolan front and back now match the vanilla skull height (0.99x), with crown and chin on the vanilla lines and ears and trunk hanging free. The Ortolan side view is the exception: it is drawn at only 0.74x, because its ear would otherwise run off the bottom of the image. **PUBLISHED cb6e354c2**; the item bookkeeping followed in `2c68a597959a`. Nothing was deployed and the bridge was not used.

**How the skull is found:** the tool removes anything thin (ears, horns, crests, eye-stalks, trunks, hair strands), keeps the largest central mass, then gives back a chin or dome tip only if it ends within a short distance. A part that keeps going, like a trunk or horn, is left out. Vanilla heads read the same either way (within 2 px). The tool can now also draw a skull overlay and a lineup beside a vanilla head at true scale. The fitter selftest passes (`run_selftests.py` GREEN). It includes made-up heads with ears hanging below the chin, horns above the crown and wide eye-stalks: the old box method misses each skull, and the new one finds crown, chin and centre within 6 px.

**Front view (south), before (6d77ea123) and after.** Skull height is a multiple of vanilla; crown and chin offsets are in vanilla head heights (+ = crown higher, chin lower; − = crown lower, chin higher).

| species | before | after | after, side / back skull height |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lasat | 0.84x, crown −0.14, chin −0.02 | 1.00x, 0.00, 0.00 | 1.02x / 0.91x |
| Mimbanese | 1.07x, crown +0.09, chin −0.01 | 1.00x, 0.00, 0.00 | 0.93x / 0.99x |
| Nelvaanian | 0.94x, crown −0.04, chin −0.02 (back chin −0.19) | 0.99x, −0.01, 0.00 | 0.90x / 0.90x |
| Ortolan | 0.70x, crown +0.13, chin −0.43 (side 0.63x with chin −0.60, back chin −0.25) | 0.99x, 0.00, 0.00 | 0.74x / 1.24x |

**The Ortolan now:** the dome fills the vanilla head box, and the trunk base and cheeks sit on the vanilla chin. Its ears hang about 0.6 of a vanilla head below the chin, over the shoulders. Two things differ from the other species:
- **Back view:** it shows a nape below the dome that the trunk hides on the front, so its skull reads 1.24x. I seated it on the front's crown line instead of the chin, using a new `--anchor north-crown` option, so the head doesn't jump up when the pawn turns around.
- **Side view:** its ear is about 25% longer than the front ear. It is recorded as a known exception in the head-limits file, which fails the check as soon as the ear is fixed, so the entry can't go stale. I filed `ORTOLAN_SIDE_EAR_REDRAW_OUTGROWS_ITS_CANVAS` to repaint the ear shorter; that is a cosmetic change, so it needs Lukas's permission first.

**Rules I loosened myself:** to leave ears free as the ruling asks, I set per-species allowances in `head_envelope.json`. Lasat: width 1.65x and 0.40 above the crown. Ortolan: width 1.55x, 0.80 below the chin, plus room for its nape. These are my numbers and Lukas may want to change them.

`HEADS_REMEASURE_ON_SKULL_NOT_EAR_TIPS` is closed at cb6e354c2. `HEADS_VERIFY_SHRUNK_TO_FIT_THE_HATS` has new item prose and a ledger note; the live check in game is still owed.

## Claude

The head refit is done and published as `cb6e354c2`. I sent you two images: the four-species lineup beside a vanilla head, and the Ortolan with its detected skull mass overlaid. Nothing is deployed, and none of it has been seen in game.

**How it works now.** The fitter strips anything thin (ears, horns, crests, eye-stalks, trunks, hair), keeps the largest central mass, and gives back a chin or dome tip only if it ends close by. Parts that keep going are left out. It also draws a skull overlay. The selftest uses made-up heads with ears below the chin, horns above the crown and wide stalks. The old bounding-box method misses each skull, and the new one finds crown, chin and centre within 6 px. All four species were refit from the original art, so resizes don't compound.

**Front view, skull height as a multiple of vanilla:**

| species | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| Lasat | 0.84× | 1.00× |
| Mimbanese | 1.07× | 1.00× |
| Nelvaanian | 0.94× | 0.99× |
| Ortolan | 0.70× | 0.99× |

**The Ortolan.** Its dome now fills the vanilla head box, with crown and chin on the vanilla lines. The ears hang about 0.6 of a head below the chin, over the shoulders.
- **Back view:** it shows a nape below the dome that the trunk hides on the front, so its skull reads 1.24×. I seated it on the front's crown line with a new `--anchor north-crown` option, so the head doesn't jump when the pawn turns around.
- **Side view:** it is drawn at only 0.74×. Its ear is about 25% longer than the front ear and would otherwise run off the image. This is recorded as a known exception in the head-limits file, and the check will fail as soon as the ear is fixed. I filed `ORTOLAN_SIDE_EAR_REDRAW_OUTGROWS_ITS_CANVAS` to repaint the ear shorter. That is a cosmetic change, so it needs your permission first.

**Allowances I chose myself.** To leave ears free as you ruled, I set per-species allowances in `head_envelope.json`. Lasat gets width up to 1.65× and 0.40 above the crown. Ortolan gets width up to 1.55× and 0.80 below the chin. These are my numbers and you may want to change them.

The new fourth agent fixes the plant tolerances patch for the 18 plants that became `RM_` twins. The three running with it are the road and river agent, the flier audit and this plant agent.

<result>I've built readable world-map styling for roads and rivers and published it. It is not deployed or checked in game: the live check is filed as `WORLD_PATHS_VERIFY_CASED_IN_GOLD`.

**What I found.** Roads are coloured lines with a dark outline. Each road type has its own width, and the colour belongs to the road's paint layer. No texture is involved, so there was nothing to generate through the art ledger. Rivers use one hard-coded grey-blue for both their edge and their water. None of the September or current mods change road or river values, so roads were vanilla then and are vanilla now. What changed:
- **The ground got paler.** The 2026-10-18 biome faces are pale enough that vanilla dirt sits within ΔE 15 of Stillsand on screen: tan on tan.
- **River colour changed.** FlowWorks' River Colours (the 2026-09-06 red → brackish → toxic gradient) joined the list on 2026-10-16. Its three anchor biomes (`AB_OcularForest`, `AB_FeraliskInfestedJungle`, `Wasteland`) match no river tile on the dev world, so rivers are coloured by elevation alone. Most come out olive-khaki on Greentide's green. My colour model predicts (171,179,131) and the in-game screenshot measures (170,182,145), which is why rivers "barely changed".
- **The highway is broken up in the record itself.** Its 153 tiles form 46 pieces of 1–9 tiles. Styling can't fix that; it needs re-routing in the record.

**What I changed.** One new file, `src/RimMandrake/BiomesShell/Source/RM_WorldPaths.cs`, plus a Mod Settings toggle "Planet: readable roads and rivers" (default on; off restores vanilla at once).

| Layer | New colour (screen) | Width (outline / fill) |
|---|---|---|
| Outline, all roads | near-black (30,24,21) | — |
| Dirt path / dirt road | umber (150,90,42) | 0.46/0.26, 0.62/0.42 |
| Stone road | slate (85,130,130) | 0.64/0.44 |
| Ancient highway | gold (250,209,0) with a dark centre line | 1.0/0.80/0.14 |

- **River widths:** creek 0.32, river 0.46, large 0.62, huge 0.82.
- **River look:** rivers now draw with the road material, so they get the same hard edge, and roads still draw over them. Their edge becomes a dark band, 0.22 times the water colour.

**Two things differ from the brief:**
- **C# instead of an XML patch.** The river colour is a hard-coded field, and the Baroque Biomes shell ships only assemblies. So there is no XML patch and no `validate_patch` run; the DLL builds clean and the settings-screen selftest passes.
- **Your river palette is kept.** The water colour stays FlowWorks' gradient. Only a river still in vanilla colour gets bright azure.

**Contrast.** Every road type and river size now clears outline contrast ≥ 3:1 and fill ΔE ≥ 20 on every biome it crosses. Vanilla fails every type on fill (ΔE 5–10). The river edges clear ≥ 3.4:1 everywhere.

**Decisions for you:**
- **Darker river colours?** The gradient's middle and end colours stay within ΔE 9.5–19 of the biomes they cross, so on those stretches only the dark edge carries the line. Darker colours in the same hues would fix it (brackish about `#1F1F00`–`#2E2E00`, toxic end about `#00293D`). That changes your palette, so I haven't made it.
- **Stale anchors.** Re-pointing them at our biomes (perhaps RM_Contagion / RM_Greentide / RM_Wasteland) is a mapping question for FlowWorks, noted in the ledger item.

The live check still needs a deploy and a load of `ASHKARR_DEV_START_2026-10-20_RECORDLINKS.rws`. The previews don't model the night side or the roads' wobble.

## Claude

The road and river agent finished and published `898817fa1`. I sent two offline previews, the desert and a coast with rivers, each with "now" on the left and "proposed" on the right. The previews don't model the night side or the roads' wobble.

**Why the roads and rivers read badly**
- **Roads:** each type is a coloured line with a dark outline and its own width. No texture is involved. The 2026-10-18 biome faces are pale enough that vanilla dirt sits within ΔE 15 of the Stillsand: tan on tan.
- **Rivers:** FlowWorks' River Colours mod joined the list on 2026-10-16. Its three anchor biomes (`AB_OcularForest`, `AB_FeraliskInfestedJungle`, `Wasteland`) match no river tile on the dev world, so rivers are coloured by elevation alone. Most come out olive-khaki on Greentide's green. The colour model predicts (171,179,131) and the in-game screenshot measures (170,182,145).
- **The highway** is broken in the record itself: 153 tiles in 46 pieces of 1 to 9 tiles. Styling can't fix that. It needs re-routing in the record.

**The change:** one new file, `RM_WorldPaths.cs`, plus a Mod Settings switch "Planet: readable roads and rivers", on by default. Off restores vanilla at once.
- **Roads:** every road gets a near-black outline.
  - Dirt is umber.
  - Stone is slate.
  - The ancient highway is gold with a dark centre line.
- **Rivers:** they now draw with the road material, so they get a hard edge, and roads still draw over them.
- **Contrast:** every road type and river size clears 3:1 outline contrast and ΔE 20 on fill on every biome it crosses. Vanilla fails every road type on fill at ΔE 5 to 10.
- **Your palette** stays. Only a river still in vanilla colour gets bright azure.

**Two decisions for you**
- **Darker river colours?** The middle and end of the FlowWorks gradient sit within ΔE 9.5 to 19 of the biomes they cross, so on those stretches only the dark edge carries the line. Darker colours in the same hues would fix it (brackish about `#1F1F00` to `#2E2E00`, toxic end about `#00293D`). That changes your palette, so I haven't made it.
- **Anchor biomes:** should the River Colours anchors be re-pointed at our biomes, perhaps RM_Contagion, RM_Greentide and RM_Wasteland? That is a mapping question for FlowWorks.

I started a live agent to do one consolidated cold load. Four agents are running: the live agent, the flier audit, the plant tolerances fix and this live agent's siblings are the two offline ones. ## Claude

The flier audit found your principle is broken in most shipped flight art. It is published as `5e2f6c135`, and I sent you the Uvak south strip as the worst-looking example. This was a read-only audit: no art or shipped frame was touched.

- **Counts:** 37 flier sets, 22 of them animated. 18 of those 22 move the body at least 2 px and at least 2% of its size between frames in at least one facing. Five sets declare frames that aren't in the repo: Kirruk, Brezak, Drexl, Thranta and Tibidee. Beldon female is three identical copies, so it has no animation at all.
- **Worst drift:** Sketto south shifts 104 px, about 1.7× its body size. PekoPeko female east shifts 133 px. FireHawk north shifts 84 px, with its head at a different height every frame. Uvak south shifts 59 px, about half its body size.
- **Cause:** the frames are mostly redrawn bodies in different poses, not registration slips. No tool in the install path registers flip-book frames on a bounding box, so the jitter is baked in when the frames are generated.
- **Sketto's body-lock never installed.** Its locked share reads 0.0004 against a floor of 0.45. So no shipped set could serve as a positive control.
- **Bounding-box tools:** five rewriting tools register on the alpha bounding box and would damage a flip-book if pointed at one: `facing_regen.py`, `facing_scale_lint.py`, `facing_set_audit.py`, `head_facing_fix.py` and `fit_head_canvas.py`. Several measuring tools also use the bounding box but never rewrite art.
- **Instrument caveat:** the figure is a mask-overlap registration of each frame's body mask. A static-body fixture reads 0.00 px and a 3 px jitter reads 3.00 px. It is approximate when wings are broad, so marginal calls (Convor, Porg, Beldon) should be judged by eye.

The fix plan is to translate frames where only position differs, and rebuild redrawn bodies with the body-plate recipe in `flyer_lock`.

**Your call:** repainting or rebuilding 18 flier sets is cosmetic art work. I haven't started it. Do you want the rebuild, and should it go worst first?

The new third agent adds guards so those five rewriting tools refuse flier flip-book inputs by name. It changes no art.

## Claude

- `Bash — grep -n -i -E "pop-?up|new faction|do nothing" design/RimMandrake/factions_unwanted_and...`
- `Bash — sed -n 68,80p design/RimMandrake/factions_unwanted_and_lingering_2026-10-20.md | cut -c...`

## Claude

This is not RimWorld adding factions by itself. It's the Vanilla Expanded Framework's "new faction" prompt, and it fires on every load of a save that hasn't been told to ignore a faction.

**What happens (from the framework's source, read from GitHub):**
- On every load, the framework checks each faction definition against the world. It offers to add any non-player faction that isn't in the world yet.
- It skips a faction in only two cases:
  - the faction is hidden and its `requiredCountAtGameStart` is 0;
  - the faction is in the save's `ignoredFactions` list.
- Clicking "Do nothing" does not add the faction to that list. So it asks again next time.
- Clicking "add" puts the faction in permanently, with settlements.

**Where we stand:** the dev saves already ignore 60 factions. Three are still offered on the current list: `GR_RoamingMonstrosities`, `VFEP_Junkers` and `VFEP_Mercenaries`. These are the pop-ups the agents keep answering "Do nothing" to. The logs show 7 pop-ups on the old 374 list, so the retirement already cut the number.

**The fix is cheap.** Add those three to the save's `ignoredFactions`, and the prompts stop for good. The shipped start save would hold every offerable absent faction there, so players never see them. That was already noted for the re-make, but I haven't filed it as work, and I haven't checked whether the live agent's world is the right place to do it.

**Your call.** Do you want any of the three in the world? If not, I'll add all three to the ignore list on the next save, plus the ban work for the factions you don't want. Tell me which, or "ignore all three".

## Lukas

Why not just make a test savegame that's past the dialog and then load it from the main menu instead?

## Claude

- `SendMessage`

## Claude

Yes, that's the right approach. A save made after the dialog is answered should carry the choice and load straight past it.

**Why it should work.** The framework skips any faction in the save's `ignoredFactions` list, and the dev saves already carry 60 of them. If answering the dialog writes the faction into that list and you then save, the next load has nothing to ask about.

**What I haven't confirmed.** The source says "Do nothing" does not add the faction to the list. If so, answering it and saving won't help, and the list needs the three added by another route.

**The test is already in progress.** The agents answered "Do nothing" and then saved the PARITY world, so loading PARITY now shows whether the pop-ups come back. I asked the live agent to report that. If they do come back, it will choose the option that writes the ignore list, re-save as `ASHKARR_DEV_START_2026-10-20_PARITY_QUIET`, and reload it once to prove the dialogs are gone.

The shipped start save should then be made the same way, so players never see these three prompts.

## Claude

I passed your caution to the flier rebuild agent and changed its plan. It must stop after the first set and show you a before/after strip before it does anything else.

**What I told it, from your words:**
- **Stop after one set.** It reports on the first set (Sketto, or the first one it finished) and holds the rest. It sends me a single before/after contact strip of all frames in one facing, plus the numbers. I relay that to you, and nothing more is installed until I pass your answer back. If it has already installed several sets, it reports those and holds the remainder.
- **Locate the body properly.** Whole-image stats, bounding boxes and a single mask are ruled out. The agent has to research how image stabilisation works. That includes OpenCV's masked registration, feature matching that treats the moving wings as outliers, phase correlation on the static regions, and optical flow of the part that moves consistently. It will pick or adapt an open-source method and cite it. It combines that with edge finding and recognising body parts such as head and eye, torso outline, and legs or tail root.
- **Fix level as well as position.** It estimates the body's rotation between frames and reports the residual angle in degrees, not just pixels.
- **Prove the locator before using it.** It must recover a deliberate known shift and rotation to under 1 px and 0.5° on synthetic bodies. It also has to be right on at least one shipped set that I verify by eye. It draws the detected body parts as an overlay on every frame, so we can see what it thinks the body is.

I saved your caution to memory too. No flight is live-tested.

I'll send you the first strip and overlay when the agent reports. Three agents are still running: the live deploy-and-verify agent, the flier rebuild and the code reviewer.

## Claude

The full-file review of `JawaBenchWorldTools.cs` found real defects but fixed none. It held back because the live deploy was still running. The game has since loaded the deployed DLL, so I've started an Opus agent to fix them in the source. It builds to staging only and does not deploy.

**Defects found**
- **D1, high:** the `range` argument is unbounded in the tile, link, mutator and feature get/set tools. `range` "0-2147483647" never ends because the loop counter overflows, and any very large range allocates gigabytes. It runs on the main thread, so it freezes the game.
- **D2, medium:** `world_links_clear` honours `to` only with exactly one tile id. With several ids it ignores `to` and wipes every link on them.
- **D3, medium:** `world_objects_set` with no field given still returns success with `changed=N`.
- **D4, low to medium:** in `world_features_set`, a mistyped `action` runs as an update and reports success. A failed `create` leaves an empty feature behind. An out-of-range `centerOnTile` is silently ignored.
- **D5, low to medium:** NaN passes every clamp in the tile set, tile import and info set tools, so a NaN can be written to the live world.
- **D6 to D9, low:**
  - Malformed tile ids are dropped silently in about eight tools.
  - Several tools report success too generously.
  - `world_features_import` with `clearExisting=false` reports false failures, and an "Infinity" size is accepted.
  - `world_links_import` with `clearFirst` deletes the old links before it checks the definitions.

Today's changes (`featuresPath`, `initialMapSize`, mutator reads) have no blocking defects. The reviewer found no lon/lat or tile-id frame errors, and the CSV parsing handles CRLF correctly.

The fix agent will add a shared tile-id parser that clamps to the grid and reports bad ids, and a finite-number check. It will confirm the tool count stays at 410, and mark the file clean only after a clean full-file re-review. Deploying it needs the game shut down, and a live smoke test comes after.

The three running are the live deploy-and-verify agent, the flier rebuild (waiting to report its first set) and this fix agent.

<result>I had already installed and published 6 of the 18 sets before your stop arrived. I've held everything after that, and nothing more goes in until you answer. All 6 now measure LOCKED in every facing. For each of them I also checked the frame strips by eye.

**What was done**
- **Rebuild tool (`flyer_cutout.py`):** per facing, one painted master frame is cut into a body part and wing parts along outlines I drew by hand. Every frame uses the same body pixels, and only the wings turn about the shoulder. Nothing is generated, so colours and identity are the master's. Frame counts are unchanged; PekoPeko female's mixed 256/512 frames are now all 512 px. Donor-cartoon frames in these sets (Uvak south 1/2/4 and east 1, PekoPeko female north 2 and south 4, Hawkbat south 2/4) were replaced from the painted master.
- **Body finder (`flier_body_stabilize.py`, new, read-only):** this answers the owner's note. It uses no whole-image stats, bounding box, centroid or opened mask. The body is the hand-drawn region, and three methods must agree:
  - **Edge-weighted alignment:** OpenCV `findTransformECC` (Evangelidis &amp; Psarakis 2008), restricted to the body.
  - **Feature matching:** SIFT points with RANSAC (`estimateAffinePartial2D`), which throws out the moving wings. This follows learnopencv's `VideoStabilization/video_stabilization.py`.
  - **Body-part templates:** front (head/shoulders) and back (hips/tail root), searched over ±12°, giving pitch on its own.
  - It reports drift in pixels and rotation in degrees, and draws the found body, the parts and the matched points on every frame.
- **Synthetic tests:** a static body reads LOCKED at ≤0.05 px/°. A body moved by (+3, −2) px and turned 4° is recovered within about 0.1 px and 0.1°. A redrawn body is called RE-POSED, so the check can fail. The rebuild, body-finder, flier-guard, frame-registration and flyer_lock selftests all pass.

**Before → after**

| set | before (old audit metric) | body finder on shipped frames | after |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sketto | 103.8 px south | RE-POSED in all 3 facings | worst 0.23 px / 0.05° (east) |
| PekoPeko female | 132.5 px east | RE-POSED east; north/south couldn't be measured (mixed canvas sizes) | ≤0.014 px / 0.006° |
| FireHawk | 84.0 px north | RE-POSED in all 3 | worst 0.08 px / 0.32° (east) |
| Uvak | 58.6 px south | RE-POSED in all 3 | ≤0.04 px / 0.03° |
| Hawkbat | 67.2 px south | RE-POSED in all 3 | ≤0.10 px / 0.11° |
| Hawkbat juvenile | 53.0 px | RE-POSED in all 3 | ≤0.04 px / 0.03° |

**Things to know**
- On the east views of PekoPeko female, FireHawk, Uvak and both Hawkbats, the body outline covers only the head and neck, so the finder checks those through the head.
- The old audit figure over-reports. Urusai, Neebray, Convor male/female (north and east) and PekoPeko male (north and south) already have pixel-still bodies, so they aren't really offenders. Convor south is a real 2–3 px slide, and PekoPeko male east mixes a 512 frame with 256 ones.

## Claude

The flier agent reported early, as you asked. Six of the 18 sets were already rebuilt and published (`842bca0`) before your stop arrived. It is holding everything else until you answer. I sent two images: Sketto south before and after, and the body finder's overlay on each rebuilt Sketto frame.

**What it did**
- **Rebuild:** per facing, one painted master frame is cut into a body part and wing parts along hand-drawn outlines. Every frame uses the same body pixels and only the wings turn about the shoulder. Nothing is generated, so colours and identity are the master's.
- **Body finder:** it uses no whole-image stats, bounding box or centroid. The body is the hand-drawn region, and three methods must agree:
  - OpenCV's masked edge-weighted alignment (`findTransformECC`).
  - SIFT feature matching with RANSAC, which throws out the moving wings. It follows learnopencv's video-stabilisation example.
  - Head/shoulder and hip/tail-root templates searched over ±12°, which give pitch on their own.

  It reports drift in pixels and rotation in degrees. A body moved by (+3, −2) px and turned 4° is recovered within about 0.1 px and 0.1°. A redrawn body is called RE-POSED, so the check can fail.

**Result**

| set | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| Sketto | 103.8 px, RE-POSED in all 3 facings | worst 0.23 px / 0.05° |
| PekoPeko female | 132.5 px | ≤0.014 px / 0.006° |
| FireHawk | 84.0 px | worst 0.08 px / 0.32° |
| Uvak | 58.6 px | ≤0.04 px / 0.03° |
| Hawkbat | 67.2 px | ≤0.10 px / 0.11° |
| Hawkbat juvenile | 53.0 px | ≤0.04 px / 0.03° |

**What to look at**
- **New wing motion:** the flapping is new, not the shipped poses, which couldn't be reused on a fixed body. Side views squash the wings, and the downstroke is a mirror image below the body.
- **Half-folded wings:** on Uvak, Hawkbat and Bogwing south the wings half-fold on the up or down stroke, because the full span leaves the canvas. These are the frames most worth your eye.
- **Cartoon frames replaced:** several donor-cartoon frames in these sets were replaced from the painted master.
- **Uvak's red wings:** its shipped art has vivid red wings, which its canon entry rules out. I kept them, because a colour change needs your permission.
- **Audit over-reported:** Urusai, Neebray, Convor and PekoPeko male are mostly already pixel-still, so they aren't really offenders. Convor south is a real 2 to 3 px slide.

**Held:** outlines are drafted for Bogwing, Krizzak, Shyrack and Whisperbird but not installed. Krizzak south and Shyrack south still go past the canvas edge. FireWasp, PekoPeko male, Beldon, Convor and the rest are not started. The five sets with missing frames are filed as `FLIER_FRAMES_FILL_FIVE_GHOST_FLOCKS`.

## Claude

The JawaBench fix agent finished and published `3966cee7a`. All nine review defects (D1 to D9) are fixed in source and built to staging only. Nothing is deployed or tried in the game.

**The nine fixes**
- **Ranges (D1):** a new helper parses every tile id. It clips ranges to the grid before allocating, with a hard cap of 250,000 ids per call, so `0-2147483647` no longer hangs the game.
- **Link clearing (D2):** `world_links_clear` refuses `to` unless exactly one tile is named.
- **Object set (D3):** `world_objects_set` refuses a call with no field, and counts `changed` only when a field actually moved.
- **Feature set (D4):** it refuses an unknown action or an off-grid `centerOnTile`, and checks everything before writing, so a failed create leaves nothing behind.
- **NaN (D5):** NaN and Infinity are refused in the tile set, tile import, tile validate, info set and feature set tools.
- **Honest success (D7):** `success` goes false on any skipped or failed row in the imports and in `world_mutators_set`. A `world_info_set` call with no fields now fails. `world_links_clear` still succeeds when there is nothing to remove, but reports `nothingRemoved=true`.
- **Feature and settlement imports (D8):** `world_features_import` no longer fails a correct run with `clearExisting=false`. It refuses an `Infinity` size and rolls back if it throws mid-write. Row numbers in settlement import errors now match the CSV's line numbers.
- **Link import (D9):** it checks every definition before `clearFirst` deletes anything.
- **Bad ids (D6):** bad or off-grid ids are named in `errors[]`. Two tool groups refuse the whole call: `world_links_set` and the object-id tools.

**Second review.** A second fresh Opus agent re-read the edited file and found six more problems, now fixed:
- `world_links_import` still deleted links for off-grid pairs before refusing them.
- The import dry runs promised clean writes they couldn't deliver.
- `world_objects_set` could stack a settlement on an occupied tile.
- The read tools reported success even when some ids failed.
- `world_view` didn't fail on a bad `centerTile` or camera jump.

**Checks:** the new offline harness passes 43 of 43, covering the argument parser only. The tool bodies have no harness, so a 12-step smoke list covers them live. The build has 0 warnings and 0 errors, and the tool count is 410, the same as the game's current DLL. Both files are marked clean.

**Deploy:** it needs the game shut down, then `build.py --gm --apply`, a relaunch and the smoke list. Filed as `WORLDTOOLS_DEPLOY_BUILT_NOT_YET_DRIVEN`. This and the label and map-size options are all waiting on the same shutdown.

| `isEnabled` | true | |
| `visibilityZoomThreshold` | Close | enum VeryClose=0, Close=1, Far=2, VeryFar=3 |

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I checked about 30 things that read the sky light, in vanilla 1.6 (through RimSage) and in our mods (by searching the source). Two need action: the night-side plants are broken, and the width of the dusk band needs a decision from the owner. Everything else behaves as you'd expect on a world with a fixed sun.

**What's fine:**
- Plant growth follows sun height. Day-side farms between 60° and 90° from the sun grow more slowly than under the old mod, which fits the "full at 30°" curve.
- Solar panels (vanilla) and our sun machines (Stillsand, mirrors, FlowWorks) never produce on the night side. On the day side they run 24 hours, so a day-side solar panel makes roughly twice vanilla's daily energy. That's a balance note, not a bug.
- The night side always counts as dark: the darkness mood penalty, darkness combat rules, auroras that never end early, and Deepfire paint glowing all apply there.
- The day side always counts as sunlit, so sanguophages can't stand outdoors there at any hour.
- Sleep, work schedules, animal rest and the clock UI run on the clock, not the light. Vanilla has no nocturnal mechanic and we have none in our mods, so sleep and schedules are unchanged.
- The sun-heat and tree-shade code works from geometry and never reads the light value.
- Of our biome darkening multipliers, only the Sump never counts as "daytime", which matches its design as permanent dusk.

**What breaks — night-side and twilight plants die:** Our plant defs set `growMinGlow 0` but leave `growOptimalGlow` at its default of 1.0. Vanilla then gives a growth factor of 0 in total darkness, so these plants never grow and die after 7.5 days. The spawner keeps planting new ones, so these biomes end up with a churn of stunted plants. Vanilla cave plants set both fields to 0, which is the fix.

| Biome | Plants that die in the dark |
|---|---|
| Sump | 10 of 10 |
| The Rot | 19 of 39 |
| Cauldron (Poison Forest) | 16 of 16 |
| Abyss | 8 of 10 |
| Gelatinous Slime | 4 of 4 |
| Wasteland | 9 of 9 (night-side tiles only) |

The new light didn't cause this; the old mod's light was also 0 on most of these tiles. It went unseen because the full mod list was loading without the tidal lock. I filed the def fix as `DARK_FLORA_FIX_WILTS_WITHOUT_A_SUN` (offline work, assigned to FOUNDRY). NEXT: list which plants each biome sheet says live without sun, then set both fields to 0 on those, and add a selftest that rejects the half-set pattern.

**Decision for a question card — how far below the horizon should dusk light reach: 6°, 12° or 18°?** The current 6° band leaves places the biome sheets call twilight completely black:
- The Sump is written as "permanent deep twilight" and sits about 10.6° below the horizon; 61% of its tiles get zero light.
- The Twilight Crags sit about 18° below; 98% of their tiles get zero light.

Real-world twilight runs 6°, 12° or 18° (civil, nautical, astronomical). At 18° the Sump gets a faint glow; at every width it still counts as dark and is too dim for plants that need sun. The slider already exists, so only the default would change. Filed as `TWILIGHT_BAND_DECIDE_HOW_FAR_DUSK_REACHES` (owner decision). NEXT: put the 6/12/18° card to him, then set the default he picks.

**Cosmetic, not filed:** day-side tiles near the terminator, with the sun below about 11°, draw faint shadows even though their shadows should be the longest. Changing that needs his permission.

I didn't search donor-mod code other than the tidal-lock mod. I made no code changes, so no selftests were run.

The full table is in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\design\RimMandrake\glow_consumers_2026-10-20.md` (after the mirror syncs; the working copy is `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry/design/RimMandrake/glow_consumers_2026-10-20.md`). The scripts and their raw output are in `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\scratch\glowconsumers\`.
