<result>The rivers were never broken in the data: our own border-blend overlay was drawn on top of them. I've fixed the code and the fix works in the running game, but the new DLL isn't deployed yet, so the next cold load would bring the dashes back.

**What was hiding them.** The border blend in `src/RimMandrake/BiomesShell/Source/RM_WorldLook.cs` drew its overlays 0.01 above the terrain. Vanilla draws rivers at exactly that height (`WorldDrawLayer_Rivers.FinalizePoint`), and the overlays block anything drawn after them at the same height. So on every biome-border tile (7,567 of them) the overlay hid the river, and the hill marks under it too. Roads sit at 0.02, which is why they drew whole. The setting being on or off made no difference, because it only changes colours and widths.

**Evidence.**
- **Links are whole:** the live link check against the record links file found 0 one-way links, 0 links to non-neighbouring tiles, and all 1,568 record links present. The 326 river links form 11 connected rivers, the same in the record and in the U4 mouth-first file. So hypotheses (a), (b) and (d) are ruled out.
- **The overlay is the cause:** on one view centred on tile 4271, I changed only the overlay height to 0.003 and redrew. The rivers went from dashes to whole lines, and the hidden hill marks reappeared.
- **Not our river restyle:** I didn't switch `RM_WorldPaths` off. It was ruled out without that test, because the dashes show with the setting off too, and the overlay change alone joined them up.

**Fix.** The overlay height is now 0.003, below the hill marks (0.005) and the rivers (0.01). It's built and committed as `e7adc75e3`, and `./publish` printed `PUBLISHED e9b8f2f4a`. That publish also carries the doc updates: the river section of `world_paths_readable_2026-10-20.md` and a new paragraph in §3 of `worldmap_in_game_audit_2026-10-20.md`, both in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\design\Jawa\worldbuilding\`. I closed `RIVER_DASHES_MEASURE_MORSE_CODE_WATER` at `e7adc75e3` and filed `RIVER_BLEND_DEPLOY_UNDER_THE_WATERLINE` for the deploy and a check after the next load. `RM_WorldLook.cs` hasn't had a code review yet.

**Why it isn't deployed.** The game holds the DLL open, so the copy was refused. The normal deploy (`--compose biomes`) would also ship 21 other changed files from other people's work. I chose not to spend a cold load on it, since the fix is already live in memory for this session. The new item covers deploying it at the next game-down.

**Images**, all in `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\scratch\riverdashes\`:

**The heights are right.** The vanilla source gives hills 0.005, rivers 0.01, and roads and pollution 0.02. Our overlays sit at 0.003, and our render queues (3502, 3503, 3505) are below hills (3510) and rivers (3520), so the dashes fix holds. The seabed layer is skipped by the `IsRootSurface` check in `RM_WorldLook.cs` and is hidden by `Patch_SeabedLayerCoplanarHide`, so it cannot z-fight with our overlays. Both settings toggles apply live: they trigger `SetAllLayersDirty`, and the next regeneration drops or rebuilds our meshes.

**Fixed:**
1. The per-layer table was a plain dictionary, so every dead terrain layer stayed pinned in memory after each world reload. It is now a `ConditionalWeakTable`.
2. A failed mesh build left partial Unity meshes undestroyed, and any already added to the layer's mesh list could still be drawn. The error path now removes them from the layer and destroys them.

**Not fixed:** `BuildSea` does a synchronous distance search of about 340 bucket lookups for each of 13 points per water tile. That should cost seconds on a planet regeneration. Shared corner points are computed three times each. I did not measure it or change it.

**Item note:** I added the review result and deploy plan to `RIVER_BLEND_DEPLOY_UNDER_THE_WATERLINE` with `rimflow note`. <result>The heads don't all follow body size: only the Lasat and Nelvaanian heads are scaled up in game. The Ortolan looking "much larger" and the Mimbanese looking "smaller" come from how the art is proportioned. I took the scaling rule from the decompiled Big and Small framework, then confirmed it by matching each shipped head PNG against the live screenshot `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\scratch\live5\PHONE_lineup_bare.png`. The bridge was taken for river work, so I used that existing screenshot rather than new pixel counts. The game launched at 18:30, after the 18:04 art install, so it shows the current art.

| species | size source | body scale | head scale (rule) | head scale (live) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lasat | HalfJotunFrame +0.75 | 1.48 | 1.369 | 1.36 |
| Nelvaanian | Big Frame gene +0.20 | 1.14 | 1.107 | 1.08–1.10 |
| Mimbanese | none | 1.00 | 1.00 | 1.00 |
| Ortolan | none (fat/hulk body types don't scale the head with default settings) | 1.00 | 1.00 | 1.00 |

Hats scale with the head. On screen, measured against a human skull:

- **Lasat:** skull 1.37 tall, 1.27 wide.
- **Nelvaanian:** skull 1.10 tall, 0.72 wide.
- **Mimbanese:** skull 1.00 tall but only 0.78 wide, on a thin body. The human beside it also has hair, which roughly doubles how big its head looks (my estimate by eye).
- **Ortolan:** skull 1.00 tall and 1.25 wide. With the ears it spans 1.52 wide and 1.64 tall, on a fat/hulk body. That matches the canon "broad rounded dome continuous with the neckless body".

**Canon:** the reference entries give no head-to-human ratio for any of the four. They only give heights: Lasat at least 2 m, Nelvaanian 1.5 m female and 2 m male, Ortolan "squat", Mimbanese no number. So the right on-screen size is your call, and I've left every target empty.

**Recommended fix:** leave the art fitted as it is and set head size in the def. The framework has a per-species head-only multiplier (`SM_HeadSize_Cosmetic`, default 1.0) that can go on each species' head gene. For example, Lasat at -0.12 gives 1.369 × 0.88 = 1.20 human skulls, which suits its 2 m+ height and stays under its 1.48 body. This touches no art or hats and can be undone in one line.

The alternative is to set a target and refit the art. A 1.0 target would shrink the Lasat art to 0.730 and the Nelvaanian to 0.903, and it means re-installing the art. Doing nothing is also reasonable, because the framework already grows heads more slowly than bodies.

**What I changed (no art touched):**
- The design doc `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\design\RimStarWars\head_envelope_2026-10-20.md` now explains the corrected model: the vanilla head box is only right for a body-size-1.0 pawn.
- `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\Utils\art\head_envelope.json` has a new per-species slot recording each species' scale and an empty on-screen target.
- `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\Utils\art\fit_head_canvas.py` uses that slot: once a target is set, `fit` and `check` size each species to it, and the line-up sheet can draw each species at its in-game scale.
- The selftest checks all of this and passes.
- An in-game-scale line-up sheet to look at is `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\scratch\headscale\lineup_onscreen.png`.

**A side finding that changes the numbers.** The five body-size genes (`RSW_BodySizeGene_*`) are cosmetic again, so a Nelvaanian looks 1.14× but plays as a size-1.0 human. You told me on 2026-08-22: "I'd like it to affect carrying capacity, food, melee, etc. just as you say. Let's use this stuff right." Commit `1c3a673fe` made them real. Then a regeneration, `7707a6aca` on 2026-08-23, silently reverted them. The comment in `BodySizeIsReal.xml` still says "now mechanical", so nobody noticed.

  - The same gap affects 13 other `_big` species and every `_small` and `_smaller` species.

The agent restores the five genes with their values and metabolism, finds the generator that rewrote them and fixes it, and adds a selftest so a regeneration can't revert them again. It recomputes every species' head scale after that, and sets the multipliers to hit those targets. It won't deploy.

  - **Dusk:** it now defaults to 12°. The Sump has 39% of tiles dark, down from 61%. The Twilight Crags have 84% dark, down from 98%.
- **Deploy state:** the Biomes package (22 files) was deployed with the game down. It includes the river fix, the dark-flora plant fix, the new Creature Behaviors DLL and the dusk change.
  - **Relaunch:** Steam never reached the bridge, so the game is still down. The live agent relaunches, loads PARITY_QUIET and runs the seasons check (pass is 40.4 °C at every sample).

## Claude

The size-genes agent finished and published `47a707a35`, with the ledger follow-up in `20e32291`. Nothing is deployed or checked in game yet.

**Size genes are real again.** All six `RSW_BodySizeGene_*` now use the mechanical size offset (big +0.2, bigger +0.5, biggest +0.7, small -0.2, smaller -0.5). The largest is 1.70, under the 1.99 giant-weapon threshold. Metabolism is back to +3 for the small genes and +5 for the smaller ones. The unused `_smallest` is real at -0.7 with metabolism +6, which is my own number; no xenotype carries it.

**Why it reverted, and the guard.** The generator `gen_races_mod.py` rebuilds those genes from the donor mod and overwrote any copy that differed. The rule now lives in a `SIZE_GENES_REAL` table in the generator, and `--check` reproduces the shipped file exactly. A new selftest fails if any of these genes is cosmetic or drifts from the table.

**Which species change.** Only eight xenotypes carry one of our size genes after the swap patch:
- **Nelvaanian:** from 1.0 to a real 1.20.
- **Seven small species:** Bothan, Defel, Gand, Geonosian variants, Selkath, Snivvian and Sullustan go from 1.0 to a real 0.80. Their hit points, carrying, food and melee move with it.
- **Raids:** raid wealth does not change for these eight, but raid difficulty does. Combat power per raid point stays the same while the Nelvaanian gets tougher and the seven small species get weaker.
- **Sprites:** they don't move, because the framework adds the mechanical and cosmetic offsets together.

**Head sizes on screen**

| species | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| Lasat | 1.369 | 1.205 (multiplier 0.88 on `RSW_LasatHead`) |
| Nelvaanian | 1.107 | 1.052 (multiplier 0.95 on `RSW_NelvaanianHead`) |
| Mimbanese | 1.00 | 1.00 |
| Ortolan | 1.00 | 1.00 |

No art was touched. Hats should shrink with the head, because the framework scales the whole head node they ride on. I read that in the decompiled code and haven't seen it in game.

**Checks:** four related selftests pass, and `validate_patch.py --live --defs` on `BodySizeIsReal.xml` reports 0 errors and 1 warning.

**Deploy.** StarWarsRaces and StarWarsPatches need `deploy_custom_mods.py --apply` at the next game-down. I added this and the 70% labels to the live agent's queue. It will line up a human with the four species, bare and hatted. It will measure the skulls with `match.py` and read the Nelvaanian's and a Bothan's body size from the running game.

<summary>Agent "Fade and shrink landmark icons" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I built the soft landmark icons and published them at `caba4437ade8e8d2178181ac433dc0c4b349a091`. Nothing has been deployed or run in the game.

**How 1.6 draws landmarks.** The draw layer (WorldDrawLayer_Landmarks) prints one quad per "Standard" landmark. The quad is the tile size × the landmark's `drawScale`, randomly rotated, using a random cell of its icon atlas. It is lifted +0.0075, or +0.025 for the landmarks that draw over roads and rivers. Each LandmarkDef has its own material on the overlay shader at render queue 3510. The engine's own `Render` already multiplies the material's alpha by the layer's `Alpha` value.

**What I changed.** The new file is `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\BiomesShell\Source\RM_WorldLandmarks.cs`; the path is the read-only mirror's, the work is in `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry`.
- **Opacity:** a Harmony postfix on the `Alpha` getter lowers it for the landmark layer only. It runs every frame, so it follows the camera and the settings at once.
- **Size:** each quad shrinks about its own centre, computed from vertices captured after vanilla's redraw. The mesh is rewritten only when the scale moves by 0.05.
- **Zooming in:** both ease back to vanilla between altitude 20% (fully soft) and 5% (vanilla), so icons can still be found up close.
- **What stays the same:** landmark data, tiles and hover/inspect text are untouched. Turning it off restores vanilla at once, with no redraw.

**Settings** (Baroque Biomes, all take effect at once):
- "Planet: soft landmark icons", default on
- icon size, default 60%
- icon opacity, default 55%
- "Full-size icons when zoomed in close", default on

**Checks.** The new selftest `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\Utils\selftest_world_landmarks.py` passes 7/7. The settings-screens selftest still passes 846/846, and winbuild built the DLL and its `.srchash` cleanly. I also added `--lm-scale` and `--lm-alpha` to `world_hill_preview.py`.

**Preview:** `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\scratch\landmarkicons\PHONE_landmarks_Rust_Cathedral_before_after.png`. The speckle is gone, but at 60% / 55% the icons are very faint. They may need tuning up once seen live.

**Two things are unproven until the live check:**
- **Fade:** it relies on the shader honouring alpha passed this way. Settlement icons take their faction tint the same way, but alpha itself is unmeasured. If icons shrink without fading, the fix is vertex alpha, which our border blend already uses on this shader.
- **Hover:** whether a landmark's name still shows on a Rust Cathedral Ruins tile.

**Landmark counts** on the Ash'karr dev start save: 3,008 landmarks across 65 defs. Ours are RUT_ComplexStructures 75, RSW_Sarlacc 8, RSW_DeadSarlacc 7, RM_TarLakes 7, RM_MagmaticQuagmire 3, RM_QuicksandPits 1 and RUT_GapingDoom 1. Six more of our defs have no tiles. The full list is in `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\scratch\landmarkicons\counts_by_def.txt`.

**Ledger.** I filed `LANDMARK_ICONS_DEPLOY_FADE_THE_CONFETTI` and closed `LANDMARK_ICONS_DECIDE_THE_REAL_SPECKLE` with `--by-card` at `caba4437a`. The item files went up at `6e6103d78` and the ledger events at `86a7a02a3`.

- That same uncommitted edit means `./sync` will refuse in the foundry clone until its owner commits it. My commits are safely on origin.

It can go out with the pending hill-decals deploy. After that, screenshot the Rust Cathedral at the speckle zoom and again zoomed in close.</result>

**Defect fixed.** The new branch only recorded the ruling when the picture had no rulings at all (`not idx.rulings_by_sha.get(sha)`). Any earlier ruling blocked it, including an agent ruling, a redo, a reject or a hold. His words were then dropped silently, and the call still returned plain `already-live`. I changed the check to `not idx.protected(sha)`, so it skips only when a real owner keep already exists. That also keeps repeats idempotent. - **Hash mismatch:** the branch only runs when the file on disk hashes to `sha`, so a ruling can't be recorded for a file that isn't the live one. A different hash takes the normal install path, and the ruling names only the new sha.

- **Push guard:** the new branch writes no `live` event, so it can't mask a missing one. This is the same as before the change.
- **Other callers:** `enact.py` passes `ruling_id`, `install_bytes`/`install_file`/`install_image` pass only `reason`, and `art.py install` and the selftests are the only `owner_said` callers. None of them reaches the new branch except `art.py`.

**One gap I left.** The new branch doesn't write a `variant` event the way the normal path does when the sha isn't in the ledger yet. That only matters for a hand-placed picture the ledger has never seen, so I didn't change it.

<result>Both defects are fixed offline. The label gate now passes against the R2 save with 0 mismatches, and the features import no longer duplicates regions. Neither change has been applied or deployed live yet.

**(1) Label drift.** The live agent's theory was wrong: the game does not recompute label centres at load. Each label's centre is saved and reloaded exactly as written. The plans and the engine simply write the same tile's centre two different ways:
- **The plans** derive it from exported latitude/longitude at radius 100. The engine's export divides by 100 even though the real centre sits at radius about 99.989, so latitude comes out slightly short, more so near the poles.
- **The engine** stores the average of the tile's corners, at that slightly smaller radius.
- **Measured on all three saves (PARITY, PARITY_QUIET, PARITY_QUIET_R2, identical results):** 57 of 71 labels miss the old 0.01-per-axis check, worst 0.178 units. As angles the gap is median 0.0045°, p90 0.0175°, max 0.109° (Slough), then 0.040° (Knuckles). Those two match the polar latitude error to within 0.0001°. On all 71, the tile nearest the saved centre is the planned tile.

No label really drifted. Both the labels70 and U1 checks (dry run and readback) now compare centres by angle, radius ignored, with a 0.4° tolerance. The closest any two neighbouring tiles get on this planet is 0.934°, so within 0.4° still means the same tile, and 0.4° is 3.7× the worst measured gap. The new selftest uses the real Slough and Knuckles values. It confirms the gap passes and that a neighbouring tile, a wrong region, a wrong size and a missing label all fail.

**Offline dry run.** I added a `--save &lt;rws&gt;` mode to the apply script. Against PARITY_QUIET_R2, labels70 "before" shows 0 mismatches and U1 "after" shows 0. As a control, U1 "before" correctly flags old centres 2–12° off.

**(2) Regions doubling.** With `clearExisting=false`, `jawa/world_features_import` now:
- Matches regions by name and updates them in place. An updated region keeps its id, type, label centre and size unless the features file overrides them. Its tiles become exactly the file's tiles, and any extras are released.
- Creates only names that don't exist yet, and refuses a name that two existing regions share.
- Has no silent default region type. It uses `Region` if that exists, otherwise it refuses and names the types already in use (e.g. `Desert x71`). No type is needed when nothing is created.
- Reports created, updated, skipped, released and unchanged counts, with success worked out from those numbers. Rollback also restores the labels of updated regions.

This logic is now Verse-free code in `JawaBenchWorldArgsCore.cs` with 13 new harness cases; the harness passes 56/56 (minimum raised to 56). The `build.py --gm` staging build had 0 errors. No tool names changed, so the count stays at 410. Nothing was deployed. The companion DLL isn't committed, so there was no `.srchash` to commit.

**PUBLISHED:**
- `4eecc31bc` — label gate and offline mode
- `3c1979382` — features import fix
- `f8a4c300c` — ledger notes, with NEXT pointers, on both items

## Claude

The label gate was the problem, not the labels. The label-and-region agent finished and published `4eecc31bc`, `3c1979382` and `f8a4c300c`. Nothing is applied or deployed live yet.

**Label drift, what it is.** The live agent's guess that the game recomputes label centres at load was wrong. Each centre is saved and reloaded exactly as written. The plans and the engine write the same tile's centre two different ways:
- **The plans** derive it from exported latitude and longitude at radius 100.
- **The engine** stores the average of the tile's corners, at about radius 99.989, so latitude comes out slightly short, more so near the poles.
- **Measured on all three saves:** 57 of 71 labels missed the old 0.01-per-axis check, worst 0.178 units. As angles the median gap is 0.0045°, p90 0.0175° and max 0.109° (Slough). On all 71, the tile nearest the saved centre is the planned tile, so no label really drifted.

**Fix.** Both the labels70 and the earlier label-fix checks now compare centres by angle, ignoring radius, with a 0.4° tolerance. The closest any two neighbouring tiles get on this planet is 0.934°, so 0.4° still means the same tile. The new selftest uses the real Slough and Knuckles values. It confirms the gap passes and that a neighbouring tile, a wrong region, a wrong size and a missing label all fail.

**Offline proof.** A new `--save` mode runs the apply script against `PARITY_QUIET_R2`. The 70% labels' before-values show 0 mismatches. As a control, the old label centres are correctly flagged 2 to 12° off.

**Regions no longer double.** With "keep existing", `world_features_import` now:
- Matches regions by name and updates them in place. An updated region keeps its id, type, label centre and size unless the file overrides them.
- Creates only names that don't exist yet, and refuses a name two existing regions share.
- Uses no silent default region type. It uses `Region` if that exists, otherwise it refuses and names the types in use.
- Reports created, updated, skipped, released and unchanged counts, with success worked out from them. Rollback also restores the labels of updated regions.

The harness passes 56 of 56, the staging build has 0 errors, and the tool count stays at 410.

<result>Stopped for handoff. Everything is committed and pushed, the bridge is released, and the game is still loading (relaunched for the deploy). The sun-through-planet fix, the companion deploy and the road/river colours are built and deployed but none of them has been checked in the running game yet.

**Sun through the planet.** The cause is Planet Atmosphere's lens flare. It is drawn on top of everything with no depth test, so its streak and halo run straight across the dark side whenever the sun sits behind the planet or just past its edge. The faint version shows even with the sun dead behind the planet; the strong version shows when the sun is just past the edge. Our stars patch and the base game's sun are not involved.
- **Fix:** I added a patch to CreatureBehaviors that blanks the flare whenever the line from the camera to the sun passes through the planet. The atmosphere, Rings and the edge glow (B) are untouched.
- **Setting:** it can be turned off under "Planet view: no sun flare through the planet" (on by default).
- **Pushed:** `b30afc395`.
- **Push detour:** `./publish` couldn't run because another agent's uncommitted FlowWorks DLL was in the way, so I pushed only my own commit onto origin/main. That agent's FlowWorks source/DLL mismatch was theirs and is now fixed upstream.

**Labels at 70%.** The dry run found 0 mismatches across 71 regions. I applied it, and the read-back found 0 mismatches. Lint showed 21 findings before and after. Closed.

**Dune Sea label.** It now sits on the tile deepest inside the region (2363, about 10.6° from the region's edge), clear of Anvil and Rust Cathedral. I checked the old position first and read it back after. The U1 label plan file records the new centre. Closed.

**Tallmere Combs camps.** The live world had no Tallmere Combs world objects (0 of 138), so nothing on the planet needed removing. The farmer camps are the ones the Leaning Scrub builds when a map is generated. I turned those off; the scavenger camps stay. The banned-factions check passes on R3. Closed at `b262d9b9f`.

**Deployed with the game down:**
- the companion DLL (built with no errors; the region-doubling fix)
- the Baroque Biomes bundle (6 files: the road/river colours from `040ec719d`, the sun-flare fix, the camps change)
